- **D-742 The measurement switches**: `CHEBY_UNWIND=off|flag|try_call` selects the unwinding backend generated code is compiled for; it is hidden, honored by release builds, and unset or empty means `off`. `CHEBY_UNWIND_EDGES=off` skips the unwind pass and forces `off`; unset or empty means on. Any other value of either is a driver error at the start of the build or REPL session, exit 1. A CLIF dump reads neither. `CHEBY_UNWIND` is temporary: U4 removes it with the `off` and `try_call` backends, and `CHEBY_UNWIND_EDGES` stays. Why: D-702 measures both backends and the pass's own cost in one binary. Extends D-702.
- **D-743 The large-frame slow path**: A function whose frame F exceeds 64 KiB is split into an outer function and an inner body. The outer entry check is `sp − (F + 64 KiB) < limit`, written so it cannot wrap. Its slow path calls `cheby_rt_stack_slow_frame(ctx, location, F)`, which handles interrupt reasons and reports an overflow against the real limit, not the interrupt-lowered one; then the outer function tail-calls the inner body. Why: D-679's guard needs the real frame size, which only a first compile gives. Refines D-679.
- **D-744 Precounted bodies**: A compiler-generated body whose counts are already in place (std intrinsics) is marked precounted. The RC pass leaves it unchanged, the unwind pass keeps its edges, and the validator checks its ownership from `Stage::Lowered` on. `clear_unwind` is allowed only in a precounted body. Why: `scope_run`'s body has a fixed RC sequence around its unwind edge that the generic pass would not produce. Extends D-693.
- **D-745 The context operand**: `Const::Ctx`, the running fiber's context, is allowed only as the first operand of a foreign call whose first C type is `ptr`; codegen passes the function's `ctx`. Why: `@std_context` kernels get `ctx` first with no thread-local lookup (D-692). Extends D-692.
- **D-746 `CHEBY_RC`**: `CHEBY_RC=naive|perceus` selects the RC mode; unset or empty means `perceus`. It is hidden, works in every build, and is read once per build and once per REPL session. Any other value is a driver error, exit 1. The two modes are identical until P1. Why: D-702 and D-718 compare RC modes in one binary. Extends D-718.
- **D-747 Thread variant trials**: Every run and `::test` trial also runs as `<name>@threads=1` and `<name>@threads=2` with the base trial's goldens; the base trial removes `CHEBY_THREADS` from the child's environment. The child environment is set in this order: `NO_COLOR=1`, the example's `env` file, `CHEBY_RT_POISON=1`, then `CHEBY_THREADS`. Skip-run examples get no variants, and blessing ignores them. When the harness itself runs with `CHEBY_THREADS=N` for another N of at least 1, the trials also run as `@threads=N`; an invalid value is a harness error, exit 2. Why: D-722 needs three thread counts, the scheduled runs at 4 threads must really run at 4, and a developer's shell must not leak into the base trial (D-444). Extends D-722.
- **D-748 `CHEBY_HARNESS_SCALE`**: A whole number from 1 to 100 (unset or empty: 1, whitespace trimmed) that multiplies the conformance (60 s), UI (180 s) and REPL (60 s) timeouts. Any other value is a harness error, exit 2. Why: emulated containers and slow machines need longer timeouts without editing the harness. Extends D-722.
- **D-749 Releasing stack pages on reuse**: A slot given back carries the fiber's lowest soft limit as its watermark. The fiber may have written down to D-457's 256 KiB headroom below it. If that reaches below the slot's warm top (128 KiB + 256 KiB below its top), every page from the usable bottom up to the warm top is released when the slot joins the free list. A fiber that never lowered its first soft limit costs no system call. Each class keeps at most 64 warm free slots; a slot given back beyond that is released whole and kept on a separate list that `take` uses only when no warm slot is free. Why: D-677 says only that pages below a watermark are released on reuse; a fiber touches stack below its soft limit without lowering it, and one mixed list kept reuse cold after a burst. Releasing at every give-back was rejected: a system call per fiber exit. The limit of 64 stays until numbers from real programs show the cost of spawn-heavy programs at one thread. Refines D-677.
- **D-750 Layout of handle objects**: Handles of kinds 40 to 43 (`Sender`, `Receiver`, `Fiber`, `Scope`) are 24-byte objects: the header, a pointer to a boxed Rust payload, and a pointer to a static per-type vtable (finalizer, type id). Every copy shares the object. The finalizer runs once, from the free worklist, when the last reference goes; it never blocks, suspends, raises or runs Cheby code, and a Rust panic in it is fatal. The selector node (44) is the header and five words, all visited as children, with no finalizer. Every handle kind behaves like an external value for `==`, hashing and debug text. Why: D-647 and D-703 fix the kinds and the finalizer timing but not the layout. Refines D-647, D-703.
- **D-751 Symbol of a large frame's inner body**: The inner body of a split large-frame function is named `cb$big$<symbol without cb$>`; the outer function keeps the original symbol, id and signature. Why: D-456 puts the kind first in every symbol, so names of different kinds cannot collide. Extends D-456, D-679.
- **D-752 No entry pad for C-ABI functions**: Every Cheby-ABI function has an entry pad after the unwind pass; C-ABI functions (entry thunks and trampolines) have none at any stage, though their calls get unwind edges. Why: the entry pad unwinds from the entry stack check, which C-ABI functions do not have (D-124). Refines D-698.
- **D-753 Count overflow marks the subgraph shared**: When an object's local count overflows and the object becomes static (D-454), everything it reaches is first marked shared. A static object then reaches only static or shared objects, and `mark_shared` keeps skipping static objects. The objects it reaches stay charged to their domain and are never freed. Why: otherwise children stayed fiber-local while other fibers could reach them. Refines D-454.
- **D-754 The slice's std subset**: Checkpoint 1 ships `std::fiber`, `std::channel` and `std::duration`. `fiber::timeout`, `spawn_with_stack` and `spawn_detached` have their final signatures; `spawn_with_stack` and `spawn_detached` end the program with `fatal runtime error: fiber::<name> is not supported yet` until L1, and `timeout` with a positive limit does the same until T1 (a limit of zero or less returns `Err(TimedOut)`, D-671). Their spec tests are on the pending list. This is temporary: T1 and L1 remove the stubs, and it never ships in a release. Why: the user chose to ship `std::duration` early and declare these functions with stub kernels rather than leave them out, so no test or golden is weakened, and T1 and L1 change only the kernels.
- **D-755 Private kernels of `std::fiber`**: `std::fiber`'s native kernels are private and `sys_`-prefixed (`sys_spawn`, `sys_join`, `sys_spawn_with_stack`, `sys_spawn_detached`) plus `take_failure`, all `@std_context`. The public functions are Cheby wrappers over them and over the `scope_run` intrinsic. Why: the runtime ABI can change without touching the public API. Extends D-648, D-693.
- **D-756 Shape of the unwind IR**: At every validation stage, unwind edges are sorted by statement, unique and before the terminator; a pad exists, is not the entry block and takes no parameters; `resume` and `clear_unwind` appear only in blocks normal flow does not reach. Before `Stage::Unwound`, a function that is not precounted has no edges, entry pad or `resume`. A function without the new forms dumps byte for byte as before. Why: the IR shape D-698 needs, checkable before the pass. Extends D-698.
- **D-757 Runtime functions declared on demand**: The runtime functions M2 adds to codegen (`StackSlowFrame`, `ScopeOpen`, `ScopeClose`, and `UnwindResume` until U4 removes it with `try_call`) are declared in a module only when a unit first uses them. Why: declaring them eagerly would renumber every later function and change the CLIF of code that does not use them, `001_hello_world.clif` included (D-262, D-273).
- **D-758 Errors in the REPL milestone header**: A transcript header takes at most one `// @milestone: M<n>` (default M1); a second one or an unknown milestone is a header error, and a header that does not parse fails its trial as a must-pass test. Blessing skips transcripts expected to fail. Why: D-726 gives the key but not its error cases. Extends D-726.
- **D-759 `bench-compile`'s RC column leaves out pad drops**: The RC-operations column of `bench-compile` counts the MIR's `dup` and `drop` statements outside unwind cleanup pads. Why: the column tracks what Perceus (P1, P2) changes on normal paths. Since the unwind pass, pad drops nearly doubled it (2642 to 4961 on the 10k `small` shape), the same under every backend, and they are not operations a program runs on its normal paths. Counting the pads too was rejected because they would dwarf what the column tracks. Refines D-718.
- **D-760 RC benchmarks against M1**: Checkpoint 1 accepts the RC group of `bench/runtime` on its geometric mean against M1, which was 0.6% to 1.6% under M1's binary at one thread in three interleaved rounds. A package after checkpoint 1 brings each RC benchmark within 3% of M1, measured the same way. At checkpoint 1, tree_inserts is about 5%, string_building about 6% and list_pipeline 3.5% to 4.5% slower than M1. Why: the user chose it. map_updates' gain of about 18% hides four benchmarks that are slower, and a geometric mean lets one benchmark's gain pay for another's loss.
- **D-761 No transparent huge pages on fiber stacks**: On Linux, the runtime marks its fiber stack regions `MADV_NOHUGEPAGE`. Its implementation is a later task. Why: with transparent huge pages on, as in Docker Desktop's Linux kernel, writing a slot's top pages makes a whole 2 MiB range resident, so a warm free slot keeps 2 MiB instead of about 384 KiB, for up to 64 warm slots per class (D-749). Refines D-677.
- **D-762 Imports that satisfaction needs in std modules**: In a module that depends only on what it imports, which is a std module (D-447), an import of a built-in type's home module is used when interface satisfaction for a built-in type in that module takes a function from that home module, as the `show` that `"{n}"` calls for an `Int` does. Without the import that satisfaction is E0063 (D-489), so W0003 does not report it. In a module of the local package, which depends on every home module implicitly, nothing changes: an import is used only through written names. Why: D-489 makes a std module import `std::int` or `std::string` to interpolate a value of that type, and D-515 then reported the import as unused, in user checks too, so `std/duration.cheby` and `std/fiber.cheby` call `int::show` and `string` functions by name instead of interpolating. D-515's reason, that satisfaction never needs an import, holds only where every home module is a dependency, and its rejected alternative hid real W0003s only there. Refines D-515.
- **D-763 The exit record**: Under the `try_call` prototype, FCB offsets 16 and 24 hold the frame pointer and return address of the caller that a generated exit stub records before it calls a C function. Rust code that calls generated code and may raise afterwards in the same kernel (`callback::call`, `cheby_rt_const_missing`) saves the record before the call and restores it after. The runtime sets the unwind flag at `ctx+12` under `try_call` too, so every catch point decides the same way under both backends. This is temporary: U4 deletes it with the `try_call` prototype (D-733). Why: the frame-pointer unwinder must find the generated frame under a runtime call, and a raise after a nested call must start from the caller's frame. Extends D-685.
- **D-764 Symbols of exit stubs**: An exit stub of the `try_call` prototype is named `cb$exit$<parameters>$<result>`, from the machine type words of its C signature (`v` for none). This is temporary: U4 deletes the stubs with the `try_call` prototype (D-733). Why: D-456 has no stub kind, and the name must be the same in every build (D-264). Extends D-456.

Several fibers on one buffered channel slow down with more workers, from contention on the channel's lock (ROB4-2); the scheduler and channel work after checkpoint 1 owns it, and the fix (a lock-free buffer, batching or tuned spinning) needs its own entry.~~ ROB4-1: Done: D-749's limit of 64 stays, with the numbers it asked for: spawn_join at one thread takes 0.63 s at 64, 0.52 s at 256 and 0.11 s at 1024, while 1024 keeps up to 384 MiB per class resident after a burst (338 MiB against 37 MiB measured); a per-worker cache of 4 slots (D-825) shipped. ## 2026-10-12: M2 checkpoint 2 plan

Goal: settle the questions that the checkpoint-2 plan (U4, U5, C1, C2) needs answered before its packages start.

- **D-765 Out of memory while marking an overflowed value shared**: When a value's local count overflows and `rc::dup_slow` marks everything it reaches as shared (D-753), the marking walk's scratch memory is not charged to the memory budget. If the system allocator refuses it, the process ends with `fatal runtime error: out of memory while marking a value shared after its count overflowed`. §11.8.2 names this as the one out-of-memory case that is not a panic. Why: `cheby_rt_dup` has no unwind edge, so it cannot raise. The case needs 2^31 references to one value and a refused allocation at once, which no real program meets. The user chose this. The rejected alternative was to mark what fits and raise `out of memory` at the fiber's next entry check. That keeps the spec as it was, but it needs a proof that no reference taken in between goes uncounted. Refines D-617, D-642, D-753.
- **D-766 Target of the compile-cost package**: Package C1 (D-735) brings build throughput under the return flag to no more than 10% below `base` (`c300fdb`) on `bench-compile`'s 100k and 1M shapes, measured as D-702 measured. 8% is reported as a stretch, and D-702's 3% stays the reference. Why: the user set it from the levers measured at planning: one walk for the unwind pass and its validation, entry unwinding through a releasing helper, and pad drops. Together they are expected to win back 3.5 to 6 of the 14 points. The rejected alternatives were 11% (safer) and 8% (it needs every lever at its upper bound). Refines D-735.
- **D-767 The never-raise kernel table stays in C1**: C1 builds a table of std kernels that never raise, so calls to them get no unwind edge. The table is cross-checked against the runtime's own list, and it does not count toward D-766's target. Why: the user chose to keep it. It removes flag loads in loops heavy on std calls, though it gives about 0 on C1's metric, because only 3 of 33,913 unwind edges in the 100k build are foreign calls. Dropping it until a measurement shows a gain was rejected. Refines D-735.
- **D-768 Runtime counters count on demand**: The event counters of `CHEBY_RT_STATS` count only when `CHEBY_RT_STATS=1` is set or a test turns them on. Allocation and byte accounting for domains, budgets and the peak (D-564, D-617, D-691) are unchanged. Why: counting on every list push and string append costs RC benchmarks 3.5 to 10 points against M1. Counting only on demand is enough on its own to bring every RC benchmark within 3% (D-760). No program can observe the counters (D-635), and `bench-run`'s stats run sets the variable. The user chose this. Refines D-635.

- ~~When the system allocator refuses an allocation halfway through an in-place update, the structure in flight leaks. U4 decides it, as the checkpoint-1 session's Open list says. The other raise path there is settled by D-765.~~ → D-771

## 2026-10-14: M2 checkpoint 2

Goal: record the user's answers at M2's checkpoint 2 (Slice+): the decisions the checkpoint's packages proposed, C1's outcome and the fix of quadratic unwind pads.

- **D-769 Being cancelled is read from the scope chain**: At four points the runtime decides whether a fiber is being cancelled from its scope chain as well as its target: a `join` that finds the joined fiber failed, a `send` or `receive` woken because the other end of its channel went away, step 3 of a scope's close, and a spawn into a scope. It walks up from the fiber's responsible scope (or the scope it closes or spawns into) through the parents, taking each scope's lock in turn, and takes the outermost scope that has a first cause or is cancelled as the fiber's target (D-667). Such a `join` then unwinds instead of panicking (D-668); such a `send` or `receive` unwinds and takes nothing, and a `send`'s value is released (D-670), even when the close came first; the close passes the cancellation through; and the spawned fiber never calls its function (D-666). Entry checks, parks and operations that complete without suspending read the target alone. A `select` woken by a closed arm follows the same rule. No spec text changes. Why: the walk reaches one scope at a time, so reading the target alone made such a fiber panic, return `Err(Nil)` or return from an inner scope at 2 or more threads but never at 1, against D-232. A probe printed `joined fiber failed` in 1 to 58 of 200 rounds at 2 to 8 threads before the change and in none after it. Both orders at a `Closed` wake are allowed by §10.7, since the fiber is still inside the suspension point when the cancellation reaches it, and this one is the same at every thread count. A walk atomic for every observer was rejected because it needs the scope and channel locks together, which the lock order forbids. Checking the chain at every entry check and park was rejected for its lock per scope level on hot paths, and because operations that complete without suspending would then deliver a cancellation, against the reading of D-102 that the runtime's tests pin. Reading the target alone, as before, was rejected because a program's output would then depend on the thread count, against D-232 and D-667's rule that an inner scope never returns to code a cancellation has reached. Refines D-666, D-667, D-668, D-670.
- **D-770 Unwinding in code compiled without edges**: With `CHEBY_UNWIND_EDGES=off`, generated code has unwind edges only in precounted bodies (D-744), and every fiber carries the setting: `cheby run`, `cheby test`, constant evaluation and the REPL alike, and a fiber inherits its spawner's. A panic, stack overflow, out of memory or deadline raised in such a fiber flushes standard output, prints its report to standard error, prints `fatal runtime error: an unwinding in code compiled without unwind edges (CHEBY_UNWIND_EDGES=off)` and exits with status 101 (D-182), never by a signal. A cancellation ends the same way without a report. The process ends once: when several fibers raise at once, the first prints and exits and the others park for good. `CHEBY_UNWIND` is read nowhere. Why: D-733 removed the `off` backend that D-742's switch forced, and under the return flag such a raise would trap with no report or run on as if nothing had happened. Keeping M1's abandon for this code would keep the interim U4 removed, and leaving it unspecified lets a measurement build print wrong output silently. Status 101 keeps the tools that run such builds on an ordinary failure, and ending once keeps the output the same at every thread count (D-232, D-722). Refines D-742, D-733.
- **D-771 A system refusal skips the leak check**: When the system allocator, not the memory budget, refuses a request, the runtime marks the accounting domain charged for it before it raises `out of memory`. After the run, the debug leak check skips a marked domain and counts the skip, and the `CHEBY_RT_STATS` report ends with `leak checks skipped: N`. A leak in a marked domain therefore never turns the exit status into 102: an uncaught refusal exits 101, and a refusal that a scope catches leaves the status the program would have had. Why: a refusal halfway through an in-place update of a list, map or text leaves the structure in flight unreachable. It leaks, but nothing is corrupted, and the program did not make the leak, so a 102 would report one it cannot fix and make `cargo xtask bless` refuse the golden (D-445). D-710 already skips and counts the check for a fiber stuck on the blocking pool. Drop guards on every in-place update were rejected for now: several hundred lines with fault-injection tests on each path, better after P2 rewrites those paths. Accepting 102 was rejected because it breaks blessing. The budget's own refusal needs no mark, because D-738's preflight releases the arguments whole. Refines D-710, D-642.
- **D-772 `cheby_rt_drop` takes the fiber's context**: The runtime symbol is `cheby_rt_drop(ctx, value)`. Generated code passes its own `ctx` at every out-of-line drop, pad drops included. A free that reaches zero reads the allocator block cache through the control block's record (D-739) and charges the domain through `ctx`, with no thread-local accessor call. A null `ctx` falls back to the thread-local lookup, which Rust callers use. Why: the drop slow path is the RC benchmarks' hottest runtime entry, and every thread-local access is an out-of-line call (D-686). A free never suspends, so D-739's rule applies. The lever was worth about 9 points on tree_inserts and 4 on list_pipeline (informative A/B). Refines D-739, D-686, D-520.
- **D-773 Exclusive accounting shards**: A domain has twice `shard_count()` shards, the exclusive ones first. A thread claims a free exclusive index the first time a fiber becomes current on it and gives it back when the thread exits. The holder is the only writer of that shard in every domain and charges it with a relaxed load and store. Host threads, fiber threads beyond the shard count and charges during a thread's teardown use a shared shard with atomic adds. Readers sum every shard. Why: D-691's shards removed contention, but each charge still paid an atomic read-modify-write, and a single writer needs none. It gained 2 to 5 points on tree_inserts in two A/B sets (informative). Refines D-691.
- **D-774 One ownership simulation in release builds**: The unwind pass's own ownership simulation checks the reference counts. An error it finds is reported as invalid MIR after reference counting, and it records the owned multiset before each unwind site. `Stage::Unwound` validation then checks that each edge's pad chain drops exactly the recorded multiset and ends in `resume`, that the entry pad drops exactly the counted parameters, and that a function that is not precounted uses only canonical pads, which leave the dominance and representation checks. A second, independent simulation runs in debug builds and every test run, and whenever `Stage::Unwound` is validated on its own. Why: D-735 found the pass and its validation a large part of the compile cost, and the validation simulated again the code the pass had just simulated. The record is the state before the instruction, so the pad check still catches a missing, extra or wrong drop and a local defined after the edge. Keeping the full simulation wherever tests run keeps D-698's independent check where it finds bugs; dropping it everywhere was rejected because the pass would then only check itself. Refines D-698.
- **D-775 Entry unwinding through a releasing helper**: When a Cheby-ABI function's entry pad chain drops at most four parameters and ends in `resume`, the entry check's slow path calls `cheby_rt_stack_slow_unwind(ctx, location, count, p0, p1, p2, p3)`, and a large frame's outer function calls `cheby_rt_stack_slow_frame_unwind(ctx, location, frame_size, count, p0..p3)` (D-743). Each runs the slow path it wraps unchanged, a cancellation's delivery included, and only if an unwinding started releases the `count` words and returns 1; otherwise it returns 0 and leaves them alone. The function branches on the result to its `resume` or its body. The helpers never raise. The MIR keeps its entry pad (D-698, D-752), and code generation translates only its `resume`. A function with five or more counted parameters keeps the translated chain. Why: entry pad chains were the largest block of code that only unwinding uses, 9,736 drop calls and about 20,000 blocks on the 100k `large` project, and the helper gained about 1.3 to 1.8 points of build throughput (informative). Flattening the chain into one block or spilling the parameters to a stack slot measured worse. Four words keep one helper signature per variant. Refines D-698, D-743, D-520.
- **D-776 The compile cost of unwinding after C1**: C1 brought the cost of unwinding to 11.0% (100k) and 13.5% (1M) of build throughput below `base` (`c300fdb`) by D-702's pooled estimator, 12.7% and 12.6% by the median of rounds, with code 18.5% larger than `base` at both sizes. That misses D-766's 10%, and the user accepts the measured cost. D-702's 3% stays the reference, and the cost is measured again after Perceus (P1 to P3). Why: the measurement took 10 interleaved rounds of `00f69d4` against `base` on an Apple M3. Nine of ten rounds at 100k and all ten at 1M miss 10%, and at 1M the miss is larger than the spread of the rounds. Against the same binary without unwind edges the cost is 11.9% and 13.3%, so it is still the pass and the code its edges generate, most of it in the back end. C1's levers won back about 1.4 to 3.1 points at 100k and 0.8 to 1.7 at 1M against D-735's 14.1% and 14.3%, less than the 3.5 to 6 that D-766 expected. Perceus changes the owned sets that the pass turns into pads, so the cost is read again after it. The alternatives were to move the target to a number today's figures meet, and a further compile-cost package with a target set before it starts, whose known levers are unmeasured, within noise or remove a release check rather than a cost. Refines D-766, D-735.
- **D-777 The pad chain's direction is chosen per function**: The unwind pass chooses each function's pad-chain direction, oldest local first or newest first, from the held sets it records, once per function before any pad is built. So a function that releases its locals oldest first shares pads as one that releases them newest first does. A regression test of about 300 lets released in order lands with it, and package U6 implements it. Why: `Pads::pad` shares pad chains as a trie built in one fixed direction, from `resume` up through the held locals oldest first. A function that releases its locals in the order it made them holds a different suffix after each release, so nothing is shared and the trie grows to about n²/2 pads. With a release `cheby`, 1000 such locals took 15.6 s and 5.2 GB, and 2000 did not finish in 7.5 minutes, against 0.32 s and 1.6 s without unwind edges (finding ROB-W3-1). The generated code was right, but generated code and long test functions can hold that many values. The direction is chosen before any pad is built, because a pad built in the wrong direction first still defeats sharing. A cap on the pads per function with a fallback release mechanism was rejected: it bounds every shape, but it is a new lowering of D-698. Leaving the cliff open was rejected too. Refines D-698.

- ~~A body that unwinds for its own scope's cancellation with no first cause fails as kind 1 with empty texts. Only a test hook reaches it until T1's timeouts (U5r's report), and T1 checks it against the spec.~~ → D-790
- ~~Package U6 lands D-777 with its regression test.~~ Done: U6 landed it in wave 1 (`843d4bc`), and D-788 refines it.
- ~~The compile cost of unwinding is measured again after P1 to P3, as D-702 measured, against `base`, with D-702's 3% as the reference (D-776).~~ Done at checkpoint 4b (PMb, quiet): after P1 to P3 (`efb067e`), build throughput is 13.0% (100k) and 13.3% (1M) below `base` by D-702's pooled estimator, and 13.1% and 13.5% by the median of rounds, against D-776's 11.0% and 13.5% and checkpoint 3's 12.9% and 13.0%; code is 18.3% and 18.5% larger than `base`. Perceus's own cost, against checkpoint 4a's tree, is 0.5% and 0.8%, inside D-702's 3%. Unwinding in the same binary (`CHEBY_UNWIND_EDGES=off`) costs 10.5% and 10.0%. One shape costs more: in a function with about a thousand or more projections of one live base, fusion's sunk `dup`s slow the register allocator, 1.47x at 2000 and 1.94x at 4000 (D-850).

## 2026-10-14: M2 checkpoint 3 plan

Goal: settle the questions that the checkpoint-3 plan (T1, L1, Perceus P1, P5 and P6, and U6) needs answered before its packages start.

- **D-778 How P1's improvement is read**: P1 (D-715) meets "the RC group improves against the baseline" when head's geometric mean over `bench/runtime`'s RC group is below M1's recorded baseline (D-718), with each RC benchmark within 3% of M1 (D-760). Each reading also reports head against the same binary under `CHEBY_RC=naive`. Why: D-718 names M1's recorded baseline as the reference, and the user chose the literal reading. Borrowing removes `dup` and `drop` pairs only at calls to private functions with handle-free parameters, so the expected gain is about 2 to 5% on tree_inserts and about 0 elsewhere, inside the 6 to 8 points that rounds spread. The target may therefore be missed and come back to the user. The rejected readings were fewer RC operations at compile time with head no slower than naive, which means "not worse" rather than "better", and a fixed gain against naive. Refines D-715, D-718.
- **D-779 Borrowing is inferred across the build's units**: The back end lowers every unit of a build, collects the facts of the whole build, infers the borrow masks, and only then counts references. Calls from a module's tests unit and from the entries unit constrain the masks of the module's private functions. So a `cheby test` build may compile a module's code with other masks than a `cheby run` build, and M3's code cache keys code by build kind. In test builds a module's code depends on its tests unit; `cheby run`'s code never depends on tests. Why: tests call private functions, often as tail calls, and the tail-call fixed point (D-715) must see every caller. Inferring per unit would let a test's call break a mask that the module's own code relies on. The rejected fallback, no borrowing in a module that has a tests unit in the build, loses the differential oracle's coverage in `::test` trials and `test-std`. The masks are private, so no other module is rebuilt when a test changes. Refines D-715, D-265.
- **D-780 Splitting large literals**: Lowering moves the rest of a list, map, interpolation or tuple literal that exceeds a statement budget into a chain of private continuations. Each continuation takes the partly built value and the free locals and tail-calls the next one, and a tuple's continuations take the tuple pre-filled with a placeholder. General splitting of long statement sequences is left for a later package. "Linear time" for P5 means that each of these literal kinds' build time grows by at most 2.5× per doubling of the element count from 50k to 200k, on a quiet machine. Why: regalloc2's cost grows superlinearly with a function's size, about 9× from 25k to 100k elements (D-643), and boxing a tuple's fields after the allocation alone still leaves a 100k-field tuple in one function. Splitting literals meets P5's goal. The user chose to defer general splitting rather than grow the checkpoint, and rejected leaving tuple literals superlinear. Refines D-720, D-643.
- **D-781 A nightly workflow for the fuzz targets**: Package P6 adds `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`, which runs the four `cargo fuzz` targets for a bounded time on a pinned dated nightly toolchain, at least 1.99-nightly. Package H1 later adds D-721's sanitizers and Miri subset to the same file. Until the branch is pushed, a local 30-minute run of the naive-versus-Perceus target, taken after borrowing is inferred, is the evidence. Why: D-718 asks for the fuzz target to run nightly, and the oracle matters most once borrowing is on. Leaving the workflow to H1 would leave P1 with no scheduled run. The pinned date keeps D-277's stable pin for every other job. Refines D-718, D-721.
- **D-782 A test's detached fibers are torn down from L1 on**: Package L1 tears down a test's detached fibers when the test ends, in `cheby test`'s sequential runner and in every build, before the test's leak count. Parallel tests, captured output and per-test domains stay with package T4. Why: example 019's `::test` trial needs it, and §13.6 already requires it in every build (D-723). Carrying 019's trials until T4 was rejected because it would leave the checkpoint's last example failing. Refines D-723.
- **D-783 A termination ends `cheby test`**: When a write or flush that has no `Result` fails during `cheby test` (D-306, D-709), the run stops at once, prints nothing more, and `cheby test` exits with status 1. §13.6 says so. Why: D-306 says that the program ends at once with status 1, and D-709 halts the runtime, so no further test could run on it. The rejected alternative failed only that test and ran the rest on a new runtime, leaking the halted one and its code. Refines D-709, D-541.
- **D-784 The text of the leaked-kinds line**: When the debug leak check fails and at least one leaked object is of a runtime kind, a second line follows D-458's line, for example `note: leaked 1 <Receiver>, and 1 object of other kinds`. It names the counts of the runtime kinds in the order Sender, Receiver, Fiber, Scope, Selector, written as their debug text. The tail `and N objects of other kinds` is left out when every leaked object is of a runtime kind. `cheby run` prints it to standard error, and `cheby test` in the failing test's leak block. No line is printed when no runtime-kind object leaked. Why: D-711 asks for the line and left its text open. Runtime kinds are the handles that are easy to leak by keeping a channel end alive, and the count of the other objects shows whether they are the whole leak. Refines D-711, D-458.
- **D-785 A write failure after the exit status is fixed**: A write failure (D-306) in a detached fiber after `main` and its scopes have finished, once standard output has been flushed and the exit status fixed, leaves that status as it is and prints nothing more. The failing fiber is frozen, and the program ends as it would have. §10.10 says so. Why: §10.10 kills detached fibers at that point, and its note says a program cannot observe the difference between killing them and tearing them down. Turning the status into 1 would make that difference observable. Refines D-709, D-073, D-306.
- **D-786 A fiber blocked inline in input or output at exit**: Until the blocking pool exists (package I1), `io::read_line` and file IO block their worker thread inside the system call. Such a call marks its fiber as in uninterruptible work. Teardown and the halt skip a marked fiber and count it, the debug leak check is then skipped and counted as for the blocking pool, and the process exits with the status already fixed. The stuck thread and the code it runs are never joined or freed. Why: neither teardown nor the halt can stop a thread inside a system call, so a program whose detached fiber waits on an input that never ends would not exit, against §10.10's item 3. D-710 already applies this rule to the blocking pool. Waiting for I1 was rejected because such programs would hang at exit until then. Refines D-710.
- **D-787 Borrowing in package-visible functions**: D-715's "module-private" means not `pub`: parameters may be borrowed in `priv` and in package-visible functions, under D-715's other conditions, and never in `pub` functions. A body edit in one module can therefore change the borrow mask, and so the ABI signature, of a package-visible function that other modules of the same package call, and M3's code cache treats those masks as something such callers depend on. Why: package visibility is the default (D-162), so ordinary code, the five RC benchmarks included, is written with it, and under the `priv`-only reading borrowing almost never applies and D-778's target would be missed. D-779's build-wide inference sees every caller in the package, so the masks stay sound. `pub` functions stay excluded because code of other packages is compiled against their ABI. The rejected alternatives were the `priv`-only reading and building both behind a switch to choose after measuring. Refines D-715, D-779.

- ~~D-725's serial group for timing-sensitive examples stays with package H1. Until then, a timing trial that fails at a gate is run again alone, and the gate item passes only when that run passes and the example's loops are clean. Both runs are reported.~~ → D-886, D-887, D-888. H1's serial group runs every trial of 009, 013 and 019 alone after the parallel batch; no timing trial failed at H1's gates.
- ~~Package U6 lands D-777 in wave 1, as D-777 schedules.~~ Done: U6 landed it in wave 1 (`843d4bc`); D-788 refines D-777.

## 2026-10-14: M2 checkpoint 3

Goal: record the user's answers at M2's checkpoint 3: the decisions the checkpoint's packages proposed, U6's nested pad chains and the reach of borrowing across a package's modules.

- **D-788 Pad chains may also nest**: Besides D-777's two directions, the unwind pass has a third pad-chain mode, nested. Sites with equal held sets share one pad. Each set's pad is built on the pad of a set holding one local fewer, tried for its newest local, then its oldest, then each other local newest first; failing that, on the pad of the larger contained set of the site before or after it, the one before on a tie; failing that, on `resume`. A pad drops what its set holds beyond that base, newest first. A function's mode is the first of ascending, descending and nested with the fewest pads, by an exact count; descending and nested are counted only when ascending would create more than 2 × (sets + locals) pads. Why: D-777's two directions do not make its own regression test linear. While a function makes its locals each site holds a prefix, and while it releases them oldest first a suffix; ascending chains share only prefixes and descending chains only suffixes, so either alone builds about n²/2 pads (44,853 blocks at n = 300). The nested mode builds 2n blocks at n = 300, 1000 and 2000, where checkpoint 2's compiler built 45,151 and 500,501 and did not finish 2000 in 120 s. The spec does not order releases during unwinding, so nothing observable changes. A function whose sites each make one local and release another, a sliding window, still gets pads quadratic in the window's width in every mode. Refines D-777, D-698.
- **D-789 Timers also fire from the monitor**: Overdue timers fire at three places: the worker that holds the poller after each poll, a worker before it searches for work, and the monitor on every tick. Firing runs no Cheby code. A fiber that a timer wakes goes to the global queue, so a lone CPU-bound fiber is preempted at the next tick (D-737) and `timeout` cancels CPU-bound work at every thread count (§10.8). Why: when every worker runs a CPU-bound fiber, no worker polls or searches, so a timer would never fire; the monitor already wakes on every tick. Measured: a woken fiber ran 8 to 12 ms after its deadline with every worker spinning, and 0.08 to 1.1 ms with idle workers. Refines D-682, D-737, D-688.
- **D-790 A timed scope's timer only cancels**: When a timed scope's timer fires, it only cancels the scope and records no first cause. `TimedOut` comes from the scope's close when the body unwound for this scope's own cancellation, which fails as kind 1 with empty texts; a body that returned before the cancellation reached it gives `Ok` (D-671). Close cancels the timer on every path out. Why: a timer target runs no Cheby code and takes no lock beyond `cancel`'s (ADR-0029), and deciding at close keeps D-671's rule that a body that returned wins. It settles the checkpoint-2 session's Open item on kind 1 with empty texts, with no spec change. Refines D-671, D-682, D-666.
- **D-791 Equal timer arms of a select**: When several `after` arms of one select share the earliest deadline, the select arms one timer and, when it fires, chooses among those arms uniformly at random. Why: one timer per select keeps the heap small and the wake path single, and D-673's uniform choice among ready arms covers equal deadlines the same way. Refines D-673, D-682.
- **D-792 Every waiting select publishes a wait node**: A select that waits publishes a cancellable wait node in every case, also with no receive arm and with no arm at all, so a cancellation, a timeout and a teardown can wake it. Why: a select with only `after` arms, or with none, could otherwise not be reached by cancellation, and an arm-less select under `fiber::timeout` must end with `TimedOut`. Refines D-695, D-670.
- **D-793 The kernels of `std::selector`**: `std::selector`'s private kernels are `sys_new`, `sys_add_receive`, `sys_add_after`, `sys_wait` and `take_selected`, all `@std_context`, bound as `cheby_selector_<name>`. `sys_wait` returns the chosen arm's mapping closure and `take_selected` hands over the received value, so the mapping function runs after every channel lock is released. Why: the naming of `std::fiber`'s and `std::channel`'s kernels (D-755), and selector.md's Mapping rule. Refines D-695, D-755.
- **D-794 A failing host random source**: If the host's random source fails, `std::random`'s kernel ends the program with a fatal runtime error that names the operating system's error. This reaches `int_between`, `fraction` and `from_host`. Why: random.md says no call fails or panics, so the failure cannot be a `Result` or a panic, and a random source that fails is a broken host, not a condition a program can handle. Refines D-656.
- **D-795 Worker slots and retiring workers**: A runtime has up to 1024 worker slots. A slot is made the first time a worker starts in it and is never emptied, so a parked or pinned fiber's worker index always names a slot, and a retired slot may restart later with a new thread. `set_thread_count` starts new workers at once in the lowest free slots, after taking back retirements not yet acted on, and retires the live workers with the highest indexes through a per-worker flag and a `YIELD` request on the fiber each runs, never `SAFEPOINT`. A retiring worker hands its runnext fiber and its deque to the global queue, leaves the idle count, wakes a parked worker to take over the poller, runs only the fibers pinned to it (D-687) until none is left, and ends. Its fiber is also asked to yield when the request races with the next resume, and when a fiber leaves its last kernel callback on a retiring worker. During a stop that is not a halt, a retiring worker stays until no fiber is runnable. Stop and halt join every worker thread, those started at run time and those retired. If the system refuses a thread, the runtime goes on with the workers it has, and `thread_count` returns the count last set. Why: fixed slots keep a fiber's worker index meaningful without remapping and need no lock on the hot path. `YIELD` lets even a lone fiber, which the monitor never preempts (D-737), give its worker back at its next entry check, as §10.2 requires of a removed thread, while `SAFEPOINT` stays the halt's. A worker that retired during a stop could otherwise strand runnable fibers. Refines D-689, D-657.
- **D-796 `[runtime]` sizes in bytes**: `[runtime]`'s `fiber-stack` and `main-stack` are stored as unrounded bytes, and the runtime rounds them up to its page size, as it rounds `spawn_with_stack`'s request. A value outside 1 MiB to 16 GiB is E0036 with ``error[E0036]: `fiber-stack` must be from 1MiB to 16GiB`` (likewise `main-stack`), and an unknown key's help says "the keys are `fiber-stack` and `main-stack`". Why: the page size is the target's, which the manifest does not know, and the message follows `memory-limit`'s form. Refines D-660.
- **D-797 Freezing only in `cheby run`**: At a normal exit, only `cheby run` in a build without the debug leak check stops the runtime by freezing its detached fibers (D-708); every other host, and every debug build, tears them down. After a termination (D-709) every host halts the runtime, then drops nothing of it and keeps the JIT's code alive until the halt has joined every thread. Why: freezing leaves memory and threads behind, which only a process about to exit can afford, and the leak check needs teardown to count down to zero (D-458). Refines D-708, D-073.
- **D-798 Each detached fiber has a base scope**: Each detached fiber gets a parentless base scope as its spawn scope, owned by the fiber and the root of every scope it opens. Teardown cancels the fiber through it. It never has members, a panic at the fiber's top level is not recorded there (its report is printed, D-669), and the fiber ends it before it retires. Why: cancellation (D-667) walks scopes, so without a base a teardown could reach neither the detached fiber nor what it started, and a base that recorded the top-level panic would turn D-669's printed report into a scope failure that nobody joins. Refines D-669, D-708, D-666.
- **D-799 Groups of detached fibers**: Detached fibers belong to a group per root: `main`'s, each test's (D-782) and, later, each REPL session's. A spawned fiber, scoped or detached, inherits its spawner's group, as it inherits its domain (D-691). A root's end does not end its group: the host tears the group down when it chooses, and the runtime tears every group down when it shuts down. A teardown stops waiting once a halt has started. Why: a test's teardown (D-782) must find every detached fiber the test started, also through its scoped and detached fibers, and a frozen fiber never leaves its group, so a teardown that met a halt would wait forever. Refines D-708, D-723, D-782.
- **D-800 A detached spawn into a closing group**: A detached spawn into a group that is being torn down starts cancelled: its function is never called, and the fiber still leaves the group when it ends. Why: otherwise a teardown could wait for a fiber that its cancellation never reached, since it was created after the walk, or the fiber could run user code during teardown (ADR-0029). It mirrors D-666's spawn into a failing scope. Refines D-666, D-669.
- **D-801 A write failure outside any entry**: A write failure on a host thread outside every fiber and entry, such as the REPL's flush of standard output before it reads a line, is not a termination: the host's read returns it as an error, and the host ends with status 1 and prints nothing more. A termination outside every entry is an internal error. Why: D-709 halts the runtime of the running entry and hands its host `Exited`; a host thread has no entry to halt and no host above it. Status 1 is D-306's. Refines D-709, D-306.
- **D-802 A root started after a termination**: Once a termination has halted a runtime, a root started on it never runs: its host gets `Exited` at once, with the status the termination fixed. Roots registered before the termination get `Exited` from the termination itself, and the first status wins. Why: a halted runtime runs nothing again (D-073, D-708), but a host that starts a root afterwards must learn how the program ended and must not wait forever. Refines D-709.
- **D-803 A termination in the std self-test**: A termination during `cargo xtask test-std` completes the halt and stops the self-test with an error that names the module, the test and the status: `the std self-test stopped: a write failed during test <module> "<name>", which ended the program with status <n>`. Why: D-783 stops `cheby test` silently with status 1, but the self-test is a developer tool whose report would otherwise end without a word. Refines D-783, D-709.
- **D-804 Waiters on a fiber blocked inline**: A teardown also skips a member of its group when every fiber of the member's subtree (the detached fiber and every fiber it started through its scopes) is parked, except at least one blocked inline in a system call, and it counts the blocked fibers. It decides so only when two polls in a row, 5 ms apart, find every remaining member in that state and no member gone between them. Why: D-786 names only the fiber in the call. A detached fiber waiting in a scope's close for a child blocked in `io::read_line` is not in the call, but after the teardown's cancellation it waits only for that call (D-667, D-670), so `cheby run` and `cheby test` would hang while the input stays open. Requiring the whole subtree to be parked keeps the teardown waiting while any fiber still unwinds, and the second poll guards against a survey that raced with a wake-up. Refines D-786, D-710.
- ~~**D-805 Later leak checks while a skipped fiber lives**: In `cheby test` and the std self-test, while a group whose teardown skipped a fiber blocked inline still has a member, every later test's debug leak check on that runtime is skipped and counted too. Once the group is empty, later tests are checked again. Why: the skipped fiber goes on when its call returns, unwinds and releases what it holds, possibly during a later test, whose delta would then hide a leak. Skipping and counting (D-710) is honest, and a group that emptied before a test started cannot release anything during it. Refines D-786, D-782, D-541.~~ Superseded by D-833.
- **D-806 When borrowing pays**: A parameter is borrowed only if no use consumes it: returned, stored in an allocation, moved, passed as a `Goto` argument, passed in an owned position of a runtime helper, passed to `list_slice` at its last use, passed in any position of a `Callee::Code` or indirect call, or passed in a direct-call position the callee does not borrow. A consuming use of a counted field projection, made after its parameter's last use, consumes the parameter too. Why: borrowing a consumed parameter adds a `dup` where counting had none, and without the projection rule borrowing would block in-place updates that naive counting allows (D-525, D-634). Refines D-715.
- **D-807 Borrowed projections**: A field projection of a borrowed value is borrowed: it holds no reference, gets a `dup` at each owned use and is never dropped. In a tail call, a borrowed projection may fill a borrowed position. Why: the parent keeps the field alive for the whole call, so the projection needs no count of its own; this is what makes borrowing pay on records and trees (tree_inserts). Refines D-715, D-072.
- **D-808 Borrowing off in the REPL and under naive counting**: D-715's "not in the REPL" covers every unit a REPL session lowers, std and package modules included. `CHEBY_RC=naive` also turns borrowing off, so naive counting stays the MIR before borrowing, the oracle of D-718. Counting a unit that borrows under options that do not is an internal error. Why: a later REPL entry could tail-call a function compiled with a mask its slot cannot change, and the differential oracle needs naive MIR free of borrowing. Refines D-715, D-718.
- **D-809 The program generator**: The program generator lives in `cheby_test_support` on stable Rust. A seeded differential test in every CI run (`rc_differential`: 40 programs, at least 30 with a borrowed parameter, and 200 seeds that must check and finish) and the nightly fuzz target `rc_differential` share it. Why: a generator only in `fuzz/` would need nightly and run only on the schedule; in the test-support crate it checks naive against Perceus on every change. Refines D-718, D-781.
- **D-810 Large tuples filled field by field**: A tuple literal of more than 32 fields is one allocation whose fields all hold the static `Nil` as a placeholder; each field is then computed, boxed and stored before the next is computed. Tuples of 32 fields or fewer keep their single allocation of all fields. The validator checks the form: the object is fresh, each field is stored once, and nothing `dup`s the object while it is filled. Storing a field never unwinds, and a pad drops the partly filled tuple, whose placeholders hold no reference. Reuse (P2) must know this form. Why: holding every field live until one allocation made compile time and frame size quadratic (68.7 s at 10k fields); field by field, only the tuple and the field being computed are live, and builds grow about 2.0× per doubling (1.86 s at 100k fields, 3.75 s at 200k). Refines D-720.
- **D-811 Continuation functions**: The rest of a large literal that lowering splits off (D-780) is a private function `FnKey::Continuation { owner, index }`, numbered per owner in creation order, apart from the owner's closures. Lowering cuts a literal once a piece has added 512 statements and blocks. A continuation takes the value built so far first, then the locals and the dictionary parameters the remaining items use. The owner calls the first continuation with an ordinary call, each continuation tail-calls the next, and the last returns; continuations are never borrowed. Its symbol has a kind of its own, `cb$cont$<module>$<owner>$<n>`, or `cb$cont$test$<module>$<ordinal>$<n>` in a test, and a REPL redefinition replaces an item's continuations with the item. Why: a kind of its own keeps continuations apart from closures and from large-frame bodies (D-679), and creation-order numbering keeps MIR and CLIF identical across runs and `--jobs` (D-264). Refines D-720, D-456, D-780.
- **D-812 Borrowing across a package's modules**: D-250's owned convention now holds at package boundaries and for `pub` functions only. Within a package, a package-visible function may be borrowed (D-787), and modules of the package that call it use its mask, so a body edit can change how they call it and they are recompiled with it. Borrowing is inferred over the build's units (D-779), not per module. Why: D-787 and D-779 changed D-250's reach without naming it, and §9, §13.12.2, §13.12.3 and ADR-0045 still stated the old rule. Refines D-250. → [ADR-0045](../adr/0045-borrowing-inferred-only-within-a-module.md)
- **D-813 Tests and other modules' masks**: In a `cheby test` build, a module's tests unit takes part in borrowing inference (D-779). A tail call from it into a package-visible function of another module can make a position of that function owned (the tail-call rule of D-807). So in test builds, editing a test can change that function's mask and rebuild the package's modules that call it. A `cheby run` build never reads a tests unit, so its code never depends on tests. M3's code cache treats a test build's masks as depending on the package's tests units. Why: D-787 let tests reach package-visible functions of other modules, which D-265's "editing a test never rebuilds dependents" and D-779's reason did not foresee. Keeping a tail call a tail call (D-015) matters more than a test build's cache. Refines D-265, D-779.

It predates checkpoint 3, and no test is carried for it. A fix changes how every live worker ends at a stop. The scheduler package S7 owns it at checkpoint 4.~~ → D-821. The next step is to separate T1n's worker slots from the extra SeqCst load per resume that the fix of CONC3-1 added.~~ Done at checkpoint 4a (S7): the slowdown was T1n's resume path, not P5b and not CONC3-1's extra load. ## 2026-10-16: M2 checkpoint 4a

Goal: record the user's answers at M2's checkpoint 4a: the decisions its packages proposed for the blocking pool and `@blocking`, the scheduler, channels and stack slots, the REPL on the runtime, parallel `cheby test` and std imports.

- **D-814 A fiber waiting on the pool keeps D-786's mark**: A fiber waiting on a blocking-pool job carries D-786's blocking mark from before it parks until the pool thread clears it, right before the wake; the fiber stores `PARKING` first, then the mark, both before it submits the job. Teardown's survey, D-804's two polls, the halt and `run_main`'s skip-and-count treat it as a fiber blocked inline. The inline path remains only for a fiber with no runtime (D-692), a refused pool thread (D-817) and a call made after a stop gave up on the pool (D-815). The completion may clear the mark after a stop or a halt began: the slot of a fiber skipped by a teardown or frozen by a halt is never retired or reused (D-708, D-786), so D-683's "must not write into the fiber's slot" reads "must not write into a slot that can be reused", and the per-worker slot cache (D-825) keeps that invariant. Why: the pool thread, not the fiber, clears the mark, so a woken fiber that waits in a run queue longer than the 5 ms poll is never skipped as blocked. Refines D-683, D-786, D-804, D-710.
- **D-815 A stop waits for fibers on the pool, then gives up**: A stop that is not a halt keeps its workers while a fiber of the runtime waits on a pool job; the completion wakes it, and the workers run it to its end, so a root that blocks during a stop reports through its `RootHandle` and `RootDone`. The stop gives up on the pool when two polls 5 ms apart find a fiber still waiting, every remaining worker idle and no job completed between them. After that a completion wakes nothing and its result is dropped with the job; its fiber is never resumed and keeps its slot, as one blocked inline (D-786); the workers end, and a later blocking call runs inline with D-786's mark. During a halt a completion wakes nothing. A stop's join takes a worker as blocked inline only when its fiber is marked and `RUNNING`. Why: a stop releases the code tokens once its workers are joined, and a fiber parked on the pool holds no worker, so a wake after the join could resume freed code; waking nothing once a stop began left a blocking root unreported (D-708's "its workers finish the fibers that are runnable"); waiting forever would hang a stop on a reader of an input that never ends. Refines D-683, D-708, D-786, D-795, D-804.
- **D-816 A cancelled fiber raises before it submits a job**: A suspending io kernel or `@blocking` call whose fiber is already targeted by a cancellation raises before any job starts: no line is consumed and no file written. A cancellation that arrives during the job waits for it (D-683); the Rust result is then dropped before any Cheby value is made, and the call raises (D-670). Why: D-670's entry check covers kernels, and checking before the submit keeps a cancelled fiber from consuming input it will never return. Refines D-670, D-683.
- **D-817 A refused pool thread**: When the system refuses a pool thread and no pool thread runs, every queued job is handed back and runs inline on its worker, with D-786's mark and the hand-off; when pool threads run, the job queues. Why: a job queued with no thread to run it would never complete; inline is the behavior D-786 already covers. Refines D-683, D-786.
- **D-818 `@blocking` calls go through a shim**: An `@blocking` call goes through a generated C-ABI shim `cb$blk$<symbol>` and `cheby_rt_blocking_call(ctx, shim, args, n)`; a later C signature for the same symbol (a REPL redefinition) gets `cb$blk$<symbol>$$<n>`. The call site spills the arguments to a stack array of words; the shim loads them by C type and stores the result widened to one word, which comes back unchanged. The call's unwind edge tests the flag at `ctx+12`, never the result, which may be 0 (D-734), and never gets the never-raise table's no-edge treatment (D-767). Each shim is defined once per JIT, with the batch that first declares it. The job holds a clone of the runtime's code tokens, which moved from `Runtime` to `Shared`, so a stop never frees code a pool thread runs (D-708). How foreign code on a pool thread builds Cheby values, and how an owned `Ptr` result of a cancelled call is released, wait for OQ-P9 (M3). Why: pool threads never run Cheby code (D-191), so the foreign function runs through a shim that takes only words. Refines D-683, D-453, D-734, D-708, D-767. Extends D-456 (the mangle kind `blk` and the `$$<n>` suffix) and D-561 (the shim's once-per-JIT rule). D-733's result test does not apply to blocking results.
- **D-819 `@blocking` with `@std_context` is E0045**: The two attributes on one function are E0045 (no new code). In either order the function keeps `@blocking` and loses `@std_context`, and the diagnostic points at both. Why: `@std_context` hands the fiber's context to the kernel, and a blocking kernel runs on a pool thread, which must never receive it (D-191, D-683). Refines D-482, D-692, D-461.
- **D-820 Last words skip the capture**: Every standard-stream write of a fiber goes to its group's capture first. A fatal runtime error and D-770's edgeless report are the exceptions: they go straight to the real standard error, never to a test's capture. Why: the process ends right after these writes, before any host could replay a capture; captured, they would be lost, and the user would see a silent exit 101. Refines D-723 ([ADR-0062](../adr/0062-parallel-tests-with-ordered-output.md)), D-770, D-684.
- **D-821 A live worker ends at a stop only when its queue is empty**: A live worker ends at a stop only under its targeted queue's lock with that queue empty, and marks its slot `DEAD`; later pushes to the slot go to the global queue. This holds for a worker that was retiring when the stop began, or leaving and rejoined, and it also waits for fibers on the pool (D-815). A fiber pinned to a worker that ended at a stop is left alone, never run, requeued or retired, with its slot and control block kept; it is counted, the stop returns, and `run_main` then skips the debug leak check and counts the skip. Outside a stop the case stays a fatal invariant error. Why: fixer-w3-1 of checkpoint 3: in the migration stress mode a stop could end a worker while another sent it a fiber. The pinned case is host API only. Keeping the worker, the rejected alternative, could hang a stop whose pinned fiber stays parked. Refines D-795, D-687. Extends D-786's skip-and-count and D-710's skipped leak check to a pinned fiber stranded at a stop.
- **D-822 Item imports that satisfaction needs**: In a std module, an import of a built-in type's home module counts as used when interface satisfaction takes a function from that module, with or without an alias, as D-762 says for module imports. An item import of the home module (`import std::int::{show}`) also makes the module a dependency; it counts as used in the same case when none of its names is written, and if one is written, its other names keep their own W0003. Modules of the local package are unchanged. Why: every import, item imports included, makes its module a dependency, and removing such an import would give E0063. Refines D-762, D-515.
- **D-823 The stdin fast path**: Standard input has one process-wide buffered reader of the runtime's own, which reads 64 KiB at a time through Rust's standard input; `io::read_line` and the REPL's prompt take their lines from it. A fiber takes a line the reader already holds on its own worker, without a pool round trip, unless a cancellation is pending (the read goes to the pool and raises there, D-816), another read waits for the reader (a pool job or the REPL's read: lines go out in the order the reads asked), or another thread holds the reader; the fiber only tries the lock. Each byte is searched for the line's end once. Why: with one pool round trip per line, 100k piped lines took 23.21x checkpoint 3's time (the measurement package MA, on a quiet machine); with the fast path, package I1t read them in 0.0548 s against checkpoint 3's 0.0642 s on a busy machine; one 64 MiB line takes 0.05 s. PMb, checkpoint 4b's quiet measurement package, confirms the 100k-line number on a quiet machine. Refines D-683 (standard input uses the pool only for reads that must wait), D-548 (the shared reader is the runtime's own, and reads are served in the order they ask), D-474 (the closed-stream layer stays under it). Extends D-731's list of measured optimizations.
- **D-824 Stack-slot counters**: `CHEBY_RT_STATS=1` prints five more lines after the event counters and before `leak checks skipped`: `stack slots taken warm`, `taken cold`, `carved`, `released whole` and `deep-released`, over every class of the global stack pool. A slot handed out is counted once, as taken warm (from a warm free list or a worker's cache), taken cold or carved. They count only when counting is on. Why: D-749's limit and the slot cache were to be judged from real programs; the counters show how often a program passes the limit. Extends D-635; D-768 applies.
- **D-825 A per-worker cache of stack slots**: Each worker keeps up to 4 free stack slots for the fibers its running fibers spawn, not counted against D-749's limit. `Worker::retire` puts there the slot of a spawned fiber it retired, after D-749's deep release, or gives it to the global pool when the cache is full. A spawn on a worker takes a slot of its class from that worker's cache first. A root started by a host takes its slot from the global pool and gives it back there. Only the worker's own thread touches its cache (never a thread-local). The worker drains its cache to the global pool before its thread ends and before its slot can be marked `DEAD`: at both halt exits, at a stop's exit and when it retires. Only a slot that may be reused enters a cache, never one a teardown skipped or a halt froze. Why: spawn-heavy programs take and give back slots under the class's lock on every spawn; MA's quiet sweep showed the cache not slower (+0.0% at 1, 2 and 4 threads), which was the user's condition for keeping it. Roots are kept out because each is on the 64 MiB main-stack class, which no spawn takes. Refines D-677, D-749. D-795's retirement and D-708's stop and halt are unchanged.
- **D-826 ROB4-2's channel fix**: `channel::send` and `receive` use the channel through the handle the caller borrows (D-187) and take their own `Arc` reference only on the path that parks (C1). A channel's state is aligned to 128 bytes, so its lock and the state it guards sit on cache lines of their own (C2). The lock, the buffer and the wait queues are D-704's, unchanged. Why: on buffered_4x4 the C2 prototype beat checkpoint 3's code by more than MA's layout band in 4 of 5 rounds at 2 threads (396.6 against 720.7 ms) and 5 of 5 at 4 threads (664.3 against 1026.0 ms), and was not slower at 1 thread (263.0 against 263.5 ms), on MA's quiet machine. A spinning lock (C3) was fastest at 4 threads but passed 2 of 5 rounds at 2 and was 7% slower at 1. PMb times the shipped tree per thread count, with the user's clause that ping_pong, sieve and spawn_join are no more than 3% slower at 1 and 2 threads. Cites D-704 as unchanged; applies D-187.
- **D-827 Every `:reload` tears down**: Every `:reload` tears the session's detached fibers down first, also with no package, no change or a rejected reload; later detached fibers join a fresh group. A group whose teardown skipped members (D-786, D-804) stays closing and is kept as stuck, and the session's end tears it down again before the runtime shuts down. Why: reloaded modules are new versions in the same session (D-625), and a frozen or running fiber of the old code could reach values the reload replaces (D-712); a closing group would start every later detached spawn cancelled (D-800). Refines D-712, D-194, D-799. Applies D-800.
- **D-828 A termination between REPL entries**: A termination (D-709) while no entry runs, such as a detached fiber's failed write, ends the session before the next prompt with the status it fixed, and prints nothing more. The REPL reads the runtime's exit status before each prompt and after each line it reads; a teardown that reports `halted` ends the session the same way. Why: D-709 hands the status to the running entry's host, and between entries there is none; a next entry on a halted runtime would get `Exited` at once (D-802). Refines D-709, D-801, D-712.
- **D-829 A session's detached fibers share standard input with the prompt**: A detached fiber of a session reads the same standard input as the prompt, and they take lines in the order they ask. A teardown at `:reload` or at session end that skips a fiber's pending read on the pool leaves the pool to take one more line when the read returns, and the fiber drops it as it unwinds. Why: one reader (D-548) cannot tell the prompt's lines from a fiber's, and a teardown cannot interrupt a read already in a system call (D-786, D-804). Refines D-548, D-194, D-683, D-712, D-804.
- **D-830 `[runtime]` is read once per session**: The manifest's `[runtime]` table (`main-stack`, `fiber-stack`) is read once, when the session starts; `:reload` does not apply a changed table, and the REPL says nothing about it. Why: D-625 compares module files only, and the session's runtime and its fiber-stack class are fixed when it starts. Refines D-625, D-660.
- **D-831 The REPL's AddressSanitizer run moves to H1**: Checkpoint 4 runs no AddressSanitizer build of the REPL. D-712's teardown is checked by `detached_reload.repl` under the harness's `CHEBY_RT_POISON=1` and by a test-only check that the channel holds no linked wait node after the teardown. The ASan run moves to H1's sanitizer jobs in `nightly.yml`. Why: the user's answer; H1 owns the sanitizer jobs (D-721, D-781). Refines D-712 (its plan row's done-when), D-721.
- **D-832 A REPL redefinition goes live after the constants it reads**: A REPL entry's batch is compiled with its same-signature redefinitions unpublished: their slots keep the old code. A slot gets its new code only once every constant that code reads, through its body and the functions it reaches, is evaluated; a new constant is evaluated only once every slot its initializer may call through, also through the initializers it forces, holds the new code. The session alternates rounds: the constants that are ready, then the slots that are ready. If neither can go on (a constant whose initializer statically reaches a redefinition whose new code reads it), every remaining slot is published first and the remaining constants are evaluated after. The first round whose constants fail ends the entry with their diagnostics, and the slots published so far are reverted; constants of later rounds are not evaluated. Why: a detached fiber (D-194) may call through a slot at any time, and D-712's "a caller sees the old word or the new one" needs the new code to be complete, constants included; before this rule a detached caller of a redefinition that read a constant reached for the first time aborted the REPL. Publishing every slot after every constant was rejected: a constant that calls a redefinition of its own entry would see the old code. Refines D-712, D-043, D-547, D-535 (within such an entry, constants are evaluated in rounds, so independent constants may come in another order than its ties), D-194.
- **D-833 A leak check per test domain**: Each test runs as a root in a fresh accounting domain that its fibers inherit. After the test's teardown, its leak check reads that domain's absolute counts, with D-784's kinds line. Only the test whose teardown skipped a fiber (D-786), or whose domain a system refusal marked (D-771), has its check skipped and counted; later tests are checked. Why: a skipped fiber that goes on when its system call returns releases what it holds into its own test's domain (D-691), which no other test's check reads, so D-805's skips of later checks are no longer needed. The user chose it. Refines D-723 ([ADR-0062](../adr/0062-parallel-tests-with-ordered-output.md)), D-786, D-691, D-771. Supersedes D-805.
- **D-834 Write failures in parallel tests**: A test's writes go to its capture and never fail. A failed write of replayed output, a result line, a failure block or the summary halts the runtime, prints nothing more and ends the run with status 1. Why: D-783 already ends `cheby test` at a write failure; with captured output it can only happen during the replay, on the host. Refines D-723, D-783, D-306, D-365, D-574, D-541.
- **D-835 Tests that run at once share process-wide state**: Tests that run at once share standard input, whose lines they take in an unspecified order, and the runtime's thread count, so one test's `runtime::set_thread_count` changes what the tests beside it observe and the bound of tests started after it (read when each test starts). Why: parallel tests run on one runtime in one process (D-723); D-004 rules out shared Cheby data, not shared process state. Refines D-723, D-548, D-795, D-658. Narrows the reach of §13.6's citation of D-004, which itself is unchanged.
- **D-836 A test refused a stack waits and retries**: A test whose root start is refused a stack waits for a running test to end and is started again; it fails, with today's text, only when no test runs. Why: with up to the thread count of tests at once, each on the main stack (D-660), a refusal while others run is transient. Refines D-723, D-660.
- **D-837 The std self-test runs one test at a time**: The std self-test runs its tests one at a time, uncaptured, each in its own accounting domain, through `cheby test`'s runner with a bound of one. Why: it writes straight to the real streams, and one runner keeps the per-test domains and checks identical. Refines D-542, D-723.
